    "Title": "Supervising the Multi-Fidelity Race of Hyperparameter Configurations",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper introduces DyHPO, a novel Bayesian Optimization method for multi-fidelity hyperparameter optimization (HPO) that dynamically allocates the HPO budget. It addresses the sub-optimal budget allocation and poor rank correlation issues in existing gray-box HPO methods. Key contributions include a new deep kernel for Gaussian Processes that embeds learning curve dynamics and a multi-budget acquisition function. DyHPO achieved statistically significant empirical gains over state-of-the-art HPO methods across 50 datasets and diverse deep learning architectures.",
    "Methodology": "DyHPO is a Bayesian Optimization approach built on Gaussian Processes (GP). It employs a novel deep kernel, K(θ, w), which uses a neural network φ to extract features from hyperparameter configurations, budget information, and the learning curve. The neural network φ consists of linear and 1D convolutional layers followed by global max pooling. The optimal kernel and neural network parameters are found by maximizing the marginal likelihood using gradient descent with Adam. A multi-fidelity Expected Improvement (EIMF) acquisition function guides the search, dynamically setting the budget for the next configuration to explore by incrementing it from the last evaluation. The algorithm iteratively selects the most promising candidate to train for a small additional budget, updating the surrogate model with new observations.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on an Amazon EC2 M5 Instance (m5.xlarge), reporting mean regret and average rank over ten repetitions. Three main benchmarks were used: LCBench (35 tabular datasets, 2,000 neural networks/dataset, 50 epochs, 7 hyperparameters), TaskSet (a subset of 12 NLP tasks with RNNs, Adam8p search space with 8 continuous hyperparameters, evaluated in 'steps'), and NAS-Bench-201 (15,625 precomputed architectures on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and ImageNet, 6 categorical hyperparameters, 200 epochs). Baselines included Random Search, Hyperband, BOHB, DEHB, ASHA, MF-DNN, and Dragonfly (BOCA). Preprocessing involved log-transforms for specific hyperparameters and Min-Max scaling, with one-hot encoding for categorical variables. The Deep Kernel GP was implemented using GPyTorch 1.5, with an RBF kernel, dense layers of 128 and 256 units, and a convolutional layer with a kernel size of three and four filters.",
    "Limitations": "The method lacks benchmarks on very large deep learning models like Transformer-based architectures, suggesting its full scalability to such models is not yet confirmed. The training procedure relies on a pause and resume mechanism, which is only applicable to parametric models and would require restarting training for non-parametric models. For small datasets where training is very fast, the computational overhead of model-based techniques like DyHPO might make simpler approaches such as random search more efficient and appealing.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research could focus on scaling HPO for very large deep learning models, such as Transformers, by developing suitable benchmarks and adapting DyHPO. Another direction involves adapting the training procedure for non-parametric models or scenarios where pause-and-resume is not feasible. Optimizing the overhead of model-based techniques for tasks with very short training times could also be explored to make DyHPO more universally applicable. The paper also invites the community to create sparse benchmarks with surrogates to save energy, implying research into more efficient benchmark design for HPO.",
    "Experiment Code": "class DyHPOAlgorithm:    def __init__(        self,        hp_candidates: np.ndarray,        log_indicator: List,        seed: int = 11,        max_benchmark_epochs: int = 52,        fantasize_step: int = 1,        minimization: bool = True,        total_budget: int = 500,        device: str = None,        dataset_name: str = 'unknown',        output_path: str = '.',        surrogate_config: dict = None,        verbose: bool = True,    ):        torch.backends.cudnn.deterministic = True        torch.backends.cudnn.benchmark = False        torch.manual_seed(seed)        np.random.seed(seed)        if device is None:            self.dev = torch.device(                'cuda') if torch.cuda.is_available() else torch.device('cpu')        else:            self.dev = torch.device(device)        self.hp_candidates = hp_candidates        self.log_indicator = log_indicator        self.scaler = MinMaxScaler()        self.hp_candidates = self.preprocess_hp_candidates()        self.minimization = minimization        self.seed = seed        if verbose:            logging_level = logging.DEBUG        else:            logging_level = logging.INFO        self.logger = logging.getLogger()        logging.basicConfig(            format='%(levelname)s:%(asctime)s:%(message)s',            filename=f'dyhpo_surrogate_{dataset_name}_{seed}.log',            level=logging_level,        )        self.examples = dict()        self.performances = dict()        torch.manual_seed(seed)        np.random.seed(seed)        self.max_benchmark_epochs = max_benchmark_epochs        self.total_budget = total_budget        self.fantasize_step = fantasize_step        self.nr_features = self.hp_candidates.shape[1]        initial_configurations_nr = 1        conf_individual_budget = 1        self.init_conf_indices = np.random.choice(self.hp_candidates.shape[0], initial_configurations_nr, replace=False)        self.init_budgets = [conf_individual_budget] * initial_configurations_nr        self.fraction_random_configs = 0.1        self.model = None        self.initial_random_index = 0        if surrogate_config is None:            self.surrogate_config = {                'nr_layers': 2,                'nr_initial_features': self.nr_features,                'layer1_units': 64,                'layer2_units': 128,                'cnn_nr_channels': 4,                'cnn_kernel_size': 3,                'batch_size': 64,                'nr_epochs': 1000,                'nr_patience_epochs': 10,                'learning_rate': 0.001,            }        else:            self.surrogate_config = surrogate_config        self.best_value_observed = np.NINF        self.diverged_configs = set()        self.info_dict = dict()        self.suggest_time_duration = 0        self.budget_spent = 0        self.output_path = output_path        self.dataset_name = dataset_name        self.no_improvement_threshold = int(self.max_benchmark_epochs + 0.2 * self.max_benchmark_epochs)        self.no_improvement_patience = 0    def _prepare_dataset_and_budgets(self) -> Dict[str, torch.Tensor]:        train_examples, train_labels, train_budgets, train_curves = self.history_configurations()        train_examples = np.array(train_examples, dtype=np.single)        train_labels = np.array(train_labels, dtype=np.single)        train_budgets = np.array(train_budgets, dtype=np.single)        train_curves = self.patch_curves_to_same_length(train_curves)        train_curves = np.array(train_curves, dtype=np.single)        train_budgets = train_budgets / self.max_benchmark_epochs        train_examples = torch.tensor(train_examples)        train_labels = torch.tensor(train_labels)        train_budgets = torch.tensor(train_budgets)        train_curves = torch.tensor(train_curves)        train_examples = train_examples.to(device=self.dev)        train_labels = train_labels.to(device=self.dev)        train_budgets = train_budgets.to(device=self.dev)        train_curves = train_curves.to(device=self.dev)        data = {            'X_train': train_examples,            'train_budgets': train_budgets,            'train_curves': train_curves,            'y_train': train_labels,        }        return data    def _train_surrogate(self):        data = self._prepare_dataset_and_budgets()        self.logger.info(f'Started training the model')        self.model.train_pipeline(            data,            load_checkpoint=False,        )    def _predict(self) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, List, List]:        configurations, hp_indices, budgets, learning_curves = self.generate_candidate_configurations()        budgets = np.array(budgets, dtype=np.single)        non_scaled_budgets = copy.deepcopy(budgets)        budgets = budgets / self.max_benchmark_epochs        configurations = np.array(configurations, dtype=np.single)        configurations = torch.tensor(configurations)        configurations = configurations.to(device=self.dev)        budgets = torch.tensor(budgets)        budgets = budgets.to(device=self.dev)        learning_curves = self.patch_curves_to_same_length(learning_curves)        learning_curves = np.array(learning_curves, dtype=np.single)        learning_curves = torch.tensor(learning_curves)        learning_curves = learning_curves.to(device=self.dev)        train_data = self._prepare_dataset_and_budgets()        test_data = {            'X_test': configurations,            'test_budgets': budgets,            'test_curves': learning_curves,        }        mean_predictions, std_predictions = self.model.predict_pipeline(train_data, test_data)        return mean_predictions, std_predictions, hp_indices, non_scaled_budgets    def suggest(self) -> Tuple[int, int]:        suggest_time_start = time.time()        if self.initial_random_index < len(self.init_conf_indices):            self.logger.info(                'Not enough configurations to build a model. '                'Returning randomly sampled configuration'            )            random_indice = self.init_conf_indices[self.initial_random_index]            budget = self.init_budgets[self.initial_random_index]            self.initial_random_index += 1            return random_indice, budget        else:            mean_predictions, std_predictions, hp_indices, non_scaled_budgets = self._predict()            best_prediction_index = self.find_suggested_config(                mean_predictions,                std_predictions,                non_scaled_budgets,            )            best_config_index = hp_indices[best_prediction_index]            if best_config_index in self.examples:                evaluated_budgets = self.examples[best_config_index]                max_budget = max(evaluated_budgets)                budget = max_budget + self.fantasize_step                if budget > self.max_benchmark_epochs:                    budget = self.max_benchmark_epochs            else:                budget = self.fantasize_step        suggest_time_end = time.time()        self.suggest_time_duration = suggest_time_end - suggest_time_start        self.budget_spent += self.fantasize_step        if self.budget_spent > self.total_budget:            exit(0)        return best_config_index, budget    def observe(        self,        hp_index: int,        b: int,        learning_curve: np.ndarray,        alg_time: Optional[float] = None,    ):        score = learning_curve[-1]        if np.isnan(learning_curve).any():            self.update_info_dict(hp_index, b, np.nan, 0)            self.diverged_configs.add(hp_index)            return        observe_time_start = time.time()        self.examples[hp_index] = np.arange(1, b + 1).tolist()        self.performances[hp_index] = learning_curve        if self.best_value_observed < score:            self.best_value_observed = score            self.no_improvement_patience = 0        else:            self.no_improvement_patience += 1        observe_time_end = time.time()        train_time_duration = 0        if self.initial_random_index >= len(self.init_conf_indices):            train_time_start = time.time()            if self.model is None:                self.model = DyHPO(                    self.surrogate_config,                    self.dev,                    self.dataset_name,                    self.output_path,                    self.seed,                )            if self.no_improvement_patience == self.no_improvement_threshold:                self.model.restart = True            self._train_surrogate()            train_time_end = time.time()            train_time_duration = train_time_end - train_time_start        observe_time_duration = observe_time_end - observe_time_start        total_duration = observe_time_duration + self.suggest_time_duration + train_time_duration        if alg_time is not None:            total_duration = total_duration + alg_time        self.update_info_dict(hp_index, b, score, total_duration)    def prepare_examples(self, hp_indices: List) -> List[np.ndarray]:        examples = []        for hp_index in hp_indices:            examples.append(self.hp_candidates[hp_index])        return examples    def generate_candidate_configurations(        self,    ) -> Tuple[List, List, List, List]:        hp_indices = []        hp_budgets = []        learning_curves = []        for hp_index in range(0, self.hp_candidates.shape[0]):            if hp_index in self.examples:                budgets = self.examples[hp_index]                max_budget = max(budgets)                next_budget = max_budget + self.fantasize_step                curve = self.performances[hp_index][:max_budget]                difference_curve_length = self.surrogate_config['cnn_kernel_size'] - len(curve)                if difference_curve_length > 0:                    curve.extend([0.0] * difference_curve_length)            else:                next_budget = self.fantasize_step                curve = [0, 0, 0]            if next_budget <= self.max_benchmark_epochs:                hp_indices.append(hp_index)                hp_budgets.append(next_budget)                learning_curves.append(curve)        configurations = self.prepare_examples(hp_indices)        return configurations, hp_indices, hp_budgets, learning_curves    def history_configurations(        self,    ) -> Tuple[List, List, List, List]:        train_examples = []        train_labels = []        train_budgets = []        train_curves = []        for hp_index in self.examples:            budgets = self.examples[hp_index]            performances = self.performances[hp_index]            example = self.hp_candidates[hp_index]            for budget, performance in zip(budgets, performances):                train_examples.append(example)                train_budgets.append(budget)                train_labels.append(performance)                train_curve = performances[:budget - 1] if budget > 1 else [0.0]                difference_curve_length = self.surrogate_config['cnn_kernel_size'] - len(train_curve)                if difference_curve_length > 0:                    train_curve.extend([0.0] * difference_curve_length)                train_curves.append(train_curve)        return train_examples, train_labels, train_budgets, train_curves    def acq(        self,        best_value: float,        mean: float,        std: float,        explore_factor: Optional[float] = 0.25,        acq_fc: str = 'ei',    ) -> float:        if acq_fc == 'ei':            if std == 0:                return 0            z = (mean - best_value) / std            acq_value = (mean - best_value) * norm.cdf(z) + std * norm.pdf(z)        elif acq_fc == 'ucb':            acq_value = mean + explore_factor * std        elif acq_fc == 'thompson':            acq_value = np.random.normal(mean, std)        elif acq_fc == 'exploit':            acq_value = mean        else:            raise NotImplementedError(                f'Acquisition function {acq_fc} has not been'                f'implemented',            )        return acq_value    def find_suggested_config(        self,        mean_predictions: np.ndarray,        mean_stds: np.ndarray,        budgets: List,    ) -> int:        highest_acq_value = np.NINF        best_index = -1        index = 0        for mean_value, std in zip(mean_predictions, mean_stds):            budget = int(budgets[index])            best_value = self.calculate_fidelity_ymax(budget)            acq_value = self.acq(best_value, mean_value, std, acq_fc='ei')            if acq_value > highest_acq_value:                highest_acq_value = acq_value                best_index = index            index += 1        return best_index    def calculate_fidelity_ymax(self, fidelity: int):        exact_fidelity_config_values = []        lower_fidelity_config_values = []        for example_index in self.examples.keys():            try:                performance = self.performances[example_index][fidelity - 1]                exact_fidelity_config_values.append(performance)            except IndexError:                learning_curve = self.performances[example_index]                lower_fidelity_config_values.append(max(learning_curve))        if len(exact_fidelity_config_values) > 0:            best_value = max(exact_fidelity_config_values)        else:            best_value = max(lower_fidelity_config_values)        return best_value    def preprocess_hp_candidates(self) -> List:        log_hp_candidates = []        for hp_candidate in self.hp_candidates:            new_hp_candidate = []            for index, hp_value in enumerate(hp_candidate):                new_hp_candidate.append(math.log(hp_value) if self.log_indicator[index] else hp_value)        log_hp_candidates = np.array(log_hp_candidates)        log_hp_candidates = self.scaler.fit_transform(log_hp_candidates)        return log_hp_candidates    @staticmethod    def patch_curves_to_same_length(curves):        max_curve_length = 0        for curve in curves:            if len(curve) > max_curve_length:                max_curve_length = len(curve)        for curve in curves:            difference = max_curve_length - len(curve)            if difference > 0:                curve.extend([0.0] * difference)        return curvesclass FeatureExtractor(nn.Module):    def __init__(self, configuration):        super(FeatureExtractor, self).__init__()        self.configuration = configuration        self.nr_layers = configuration['nr_layers']        self.act_func = nn.LeakyReLU()        initial_features = configuration['nr_initial_features'] + 1        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(initial_features, configuration['layer1_units'])        self.bn1 = nn.BatchNorm1d(configuration['layer1_units'])        for i in range(2, self.nr_layers):            setattr(                self,                f'fc{i + 1}',                nn.Linear(configuration[f'layer{i - 1}_units'], configuration[f'layer{i}_units']),            )            setattr(                self,                f'bn{i + 1}',                nn.BatchNorm1d(configuration[f'layer{i}_units']),            )        setattr(            self,            f'fc{self.nr_layers}',            nn.Linear(                configuration[f'layer{self.nr_layers - 1}_units'] +                configuration['cnn_nr_channels'],                configuration[f'layer{self.nr_layers}_units']            ),        )        self.cnn = nn.Sequential(            nn.Conv1d(in_channels=1, kernel_size=(configuration['cnn_kernel_size'],), out_channels=4),            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool1d(1),        )    def forward(self, x, budgets, learning_curves):        budgets = torch.unsqueeze(budgets, dim=1)        x = cat((x, budgets), dim=1)        x = self.fc1(x)        x = self.act_func(self.bn1(x))        for i in range(2, self.nr_layers):            x = self.act_func(                getattr(self, f'bn{i}')(                    getattr(self, f'fc{i}')(                        x                    )                )            )        learning_curves = torch.unsqueeze(learning_curves, 1)        lc_features = self.cnn(learning_curves)        lc_features = torch.squeeze(lc_features, 2)        x = cat((x, lc_features), dim=1)        x = self.act_func(getattr(self, f'fc{self.nr_layers}')(x))        return xclass GPRegressionModel(gpytorch.models.ExactGP):    def __init__(        self,        train_x: torch.Tensor,        train_y: torch.Tensor,        likelihood: gpytorch.likelihoods.GaussianLikelihood,    ):        super(GPRegressionModel, self).__init__(train_x, train_y, likelihood)        self.mean_module = gpytorch.means.ConstantMean()        self.covar_module = gpytorch.kernels.ScaleKernel(gpytorch.kernels.RBFKernel())    def forward(self, x):        mean_x = self.mean_module(x)        covar_x = self.covar_module(x)        return gpytorch.distributions.MultivariateNormal(mean_x, covar_x)class DyHPO:    def __init__(        self,        configuration: Dict,        device: torch.device,        dataset_name: str = 'unknown',        output_path: str = '.',        seed: int = 11,    ):        super(DyHPO, self).__init__()        self.feature_extractor = FeatureExtractor(configuration)        self.batch_size = configuration['batch_size']        self.nr_epochs = configuration['nr_epochs']        self.early_stopping_patience = configuration['nr_patience_epochs']        self.refine_epochs = 50        self.dev = device        self.seed = seed        self.model, self.likelihood, self.mll = \r            self.get_model_likelihood_mll(                configuration[f'layer{self.feature_extractor.nr_layers}_units']            )        self.model.to(self.dev)        self.likelihood.to(self.dev)        self.feature_extractor.to(self.dev)        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([            {'params': self.model.parameters(), 'lr': configuration['learning_rate']},            {'params': self.feature_extractor.parameters(), 'lr': configuration['learning_rate']}],        )        self.configuration = configuration        self.initial_nr_points = 10        self.iterations = 0        self.restart = True        self.logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)        self.checkpoint_path = os.path.join(            output_path,            'checkpoints',            f'{dataset_name}',            f'{self.seed}',        )        os.makedirs(self.checkpoint_path, exist_ok=True)        self.checkpoint_file = os.path.join(            self.checkpoint_path,            'checkpoint.pth'        )    def restart_optimization(self):        self.feature_extractor = FeatureExtractor(self.configuration).to(self.dev)        self.model, self.likelihood, self.mll = \r            self.get_model_likelihood_mll(                self.configuration[f'layer{self.feature_extractor.nr_layers}_units'],            )        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([            {'params': self.model.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']},            {'params': self.feature_extractor.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']}],        )    def get_model_likelihood_mll(        self,        train_size: int,    ) -> Tuple[GPRegressionModel, gpytorch.likelihoods.GaussianLikelihood, gpytorch.mlls.ExactMarginalLogLikelihood]:        train_x = torch.ones(train_size, train_size).to(self.dev)        train_y = torch.ones(train_size).to(self.dev)        likelihood = gpytorch.likelihoods.GaussianLikelihood().to(self.dev)        model = GPRegressionModel(train_x=train_x, train_y=train_y, likelihood=likelihood).to(self.dev)        mll = gpytorch.mlls.ExactMarginalLogLikelihood(likelihood, model).to(self.dev)        return model, likelihood, mll    def train_pipeline(self, data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor], load_checkpoint: bool = False):        self.iterations += 1        self.logger.debug(f'Starting iteration: {self.iterations}')        weights_changed = False        if load_checkpoint:            try:                self.load_checkpoint()            except FileNotFoundError:                self.logger.error(f'No checkpoint file found at: {self.checkpoint_file}'                                  f'Training the GP from the beginning')        self.model.train()        self.likelihood.train()        self.feature_extractor.train()        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([            {'params': self.model.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']},            {'params': self.feature_extractor.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']}],        )        X_train = data['X_train']        train_budgets = data['train_budgets']        train_curves = data['train_curves']        y_train = data['y_train']        initial_state = self.get_state()        training_errored = False        if self.restart:            self.restart_optimization()            nr_epochs = self.nr_epochs            if self.initial_nr_points <= self.iterations:                self.restart = False        else:            nr_epochs = self.refine_epochs        mse = 0.0        for epoch_nr in range(0, nr_epochs):            nr_examples_batch = X_train.size(dim=0)            if nr_examples_batch == 1:                continue            self.optimizer.zero_grad()            projected_x = self.feature_extractor(X_train, train_budgets, train_curves)            self.model.set_train_data(projected_x, y_train, strict=False)            output = self.model(projected_x)            try:                loss = -self.mll(output, self.model.train_targets)                loss_value = loss.detach().to('cpu').item()                mse = gpytorch.metrics.mean_squared_error(output, self.model.train_targets)                self.logger.debug(                    f'Epoch {epoch_nr} - MSE {mse:.5f}, '                    f'Loss: {loss_value:.3f}, '                    f'lengthscale: {self.model.covar_module.base_kernel.lengthscale.item():.3f}, '                    f'noise: {self.model.likelihood.noise.item():.3f}, '                )                loss.backward()                self.optimizer.step()            except Exception as training_error:                self.logger.error(f'The following error happened while training: {training_error}')                self.restart = True                training_errored = True                break        if training_errored:            self.save_checkpoint(initial_state)            self.load_checkpoint()    def predict_pipeline(        self,        train_data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor],        test_data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor],    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:        self.model.eval()        self.feature_extractor.eval()        self.likelihood.eval()        with torch.no_grad():            projected_train_x = self.feature_extractor(                train_data['X_train'],                train_data['train_budgets'],                train_data['train_curves'],            )            self.model.set_train_data(inputs=projected_train_x, targets=train_data['y_train'], strict=False)            projected_test_x = self.feature_extractor(                test_data['X_test'],                test_data['test_budgets'],                test_data['test_curves'],            )            preds = self.likelihood(self.model(projected_test_x))        means = preds.mean.detach().to('cpu').numpy().reshape(-1, )        stds = preds.stddev.detach().to('cpu').numpy().reshape(-1, )        return means, stds    def load_checkpoint(self):        checkpoint = torch.load(self.checkpoint_file)        self.model.load_state_dict(checkpoint['gp_state_dict'])        self.feature_extractor.load_state_dict(checkpoint['feature_extractor_state_dict'])        self.likelihood.load_state_dict(checkpoint['likelihood_state_dict'])    def save_checkpoint(self, state: Dict =None):        if state is None:            torch.save(                self.get_state(),                self.checkpoint_file,            )        else:            torch.save(                state,                self.checkpoint_file,            )    def get_state(self) -> Dict[str, Dict]:        current_state = {            'gp_state_dict': deepcopy(self.model.state_dict()),            'feature_extractor_state_dict': deepcopy(self.feature_extractor.state_dict()),            'likelihood_state_dict': deepcopy(self.likelihood.state_dict()),        }        return current_state",
    "Experiment Result": "Optimization Algorithm: DyHPO (Bayesian Optimization with Deep Kernel Gaussian Process).Surrogate Model: DyHPO (Deep Gaussian Process) comprised of:\n  - Feature Extractor (Neural Network φ):\n    - Architecture: Linear and 1D Convolutional layers followed by Global Max Pooling.\n    - Number of layers (excluding CNN): 2 (controlled by `nr_layers`).\n    - Input features: `nr_initial_features` (determined by `hp_candidates.shape[1]`) + 1 (for concatenated budget).\n    - Layer 1 units: 64.\n    - Layer 2 units: 128.\n    - CNN channels: 4.\n    - CNN kernel size: 3.\n  - GP Regression Model:\n    - Mean module: ConstantMean.\n    - Covariance module: ScaleKernel(RBFKernel()).\nTraining:\n  - Optimizer: Adam.\n  - Learning rate: 0.001.\n  - Batch size: 64.\n  - Number of epochs (`nr_epochs`): 1000 for full training (initial or restart), 50 for refinement (`refine_epochs`).\n  - Early stopping patience (`nr_patience_epochs`): 10 epochs (not directly used for early stopping but mentioned in config).\n  - Model restart: Triggered if `no_improvement_patience` reaches `no_improvement_threshold` (`max_benchmark_epochs + 0.2 * max_benchmark_epochs`), or if training errors.\n  - Initial training points: Full training is performed until `initial_nr_points` (default 10) iterations or when model `restart` is False.\nAcquisition Function: Expected Improvement ('ei') is used.\nBudget Allocation:\n  - `fantasize_step`: 1 (budget increment for next evaluation).\n  - `total_budget`: 500 (maximum budget for HPO).\nInitial Exploration:\n  - `initial_configurations_nr`: 1 (number of random configurations to evaluate initially).\n  - `conf_individual_budget`: 1 (budget for each initial random configuration).\n  - `fraction_random_configs`: 0.1 (percentage of configurations taken randomly instead of sampled from model - currently not actively used in `suggest` beyond initial configurations).\nHyperparameter Preprocessing:\n  - Log transformation applied to hyperparameters indicated by `log_indicator`.\n  - MinMaxScaler applied to all preprocessed hyperparameter candidates.\nProblem Type: Minimization (default `True`).\nSeed: 11 (default).\nMaximum Benchmark Epochs (`max_benchmark_epochs`): 52 (maximum fidelity/budget a configuration can be evaluated up to).\nDevice: CUDA if available, otherwise CPU."
}{
    "Title": "Supervising the Multi-Fidelity Race of Hyperparameter Configurations",
    "Main Contributions": "DyHPO, a Bayesian Optimization method, for dynamic multi-fidelity hyperparameter optimization (HPO). It introduces a novel deep kernel for Gaussian Processes that embeds learning curve dynamics and an acquisition function (EIMF) that incorporates multi-budget information. DyHPO dynamically allocates HPO budget, outperforming state-of-the-art methods significantly across 50 datasets and diverse architectures (MLP, CNN/NAS, RNN).",
    "Methodology": "DyHPO utilizes a Gaussian Process (GP) surrogate model with a deep kernel. The kernel's feature extractor (φ) is a neural network, processing normalized budget and hyperparameter configurations through linear layers, and learning curves through one-dimensional convolutions followed by global max pooling. Parameters are optimized via maximum likelihood using Adam. A Multi-Fidelity Expected Improvement (EIMF) acquisition function dynamically selects promising configurations for incremental training.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on an Amazon EC2 M5 instance, reporting mean regret and average rank over ten repetitions. Benchmarks included LCBench (35 tabular datasets, 50 epochs), TaskSet (12 NLP tasks, 1000 steps), and NAS-Bench-201 (CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, ImageNet, 200 epochs). Baselines were Random Search, HyperBand, BOHB, DEHB, ASHA, MF-DNN, and Dragonfly (BOCA). Preprocessing involved log-transforms and min-max scaling for hyperparameters. An ablation study assessed the impact of the learning curve input.",
    "Limitations": "The method lacks evaluation on very large deep learning models, such as Transformer-based architectures, due to the unavailability of suitable tabular benchmarks. The 'pause and resume' training procedure is only applicable to parametric models, requiring restarts for others. For small, fast-training datasets, the overhead of model-based techniques like DyHPO may make simpler approaches like random search more appealing. The paper also warns against running for extended periods for marginal gains unless critical.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work includes the development of tabular benchmarks for very large deep learning models (e.g., Transformers) to further scale HPO. The paper also implicitly suggests the community should focus on creating sparse benchmarks with surrogates to conserve energy, in contrast to dense tabular ones.",

self.nr_layers):            x = self.act_func(                getattr(self, f'bn{i}')(                    getattr(self, f'fc{i}')(                        x                    )                )            )        learning_curves = torch.unsqueeze(learning_curves, 1)        lc_features = self.cnn(learning_curves)        lc_features = torch.squeeze(lc_features, 2)        x = cat((x, lc_features), dim=1)        x = self.act_func(getattr(self, f'fc{self.nr_layers}')(x))        return x\n\nclass GPRegressionModel(gpytorch.models.ExactGP):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        train_x: torch.Tensor,\n        train_y: torch.Tensor,\n        likelihood: gpytorch.likelihoods.GaussianLikelihood,\n    ):\n        super(GPRegressionModel, self).__init__(train_x, train_y, likelihood)\n\n        self.mean_module = gpytorch.means.ConstantMean()\n        self.covar_module = gpytorch.kernels.ScaleKernel(gpytorch.kernels.RBFKernel())\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n\n        mean_x = self.mean_module(x)\n        covar_x = self.covar_module(x)\n\n        return gpytorch.distributions.MultivariateNormal(mean_x, covar_x)\n\nclass DyHPO:\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        configuration: Dict,\n        device: torch.device,\n        dataset_name: str = 'unknown',\n        output_path: str = '.',\n        seed: int = 11,\n    ):\n        super(DyHPO, self).__init__()\n        self.feature_extractor = FeatureExtractor(configuration)\n        self.batch_size = configuration['batch_size']\n        self.nr_epochs = configuration['nr_epochs']\n        self.early_stopping_patience = configuration['nr_patience_epochs']\n        self.refine_epochs = 50\n        self.dev = device\n        self.seed = seed\n        self.model, self.likelihood, self.mll = \\\n            self.get_model_likelihood_mll(\n                configuration[f'layer{self.feature_extractor.nr_layers}_units']\n            )\n\n        self.model.to(self.dev)\n        self.likelihood.to(self.dev)\n        self.feature_extractor.to(self.dev)\n\n        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([\n            {'params': self.model.parameters(), 'lr': configuration['learning_rate']},\n            {'params': self.feature_extractor.parameters(), 'lr': configuration['learning_rate']}],\n        )\n\n        self.configuration = configuration\n        self.initial_nr_points = 10\n        self.iterations = 0\n        self.restart = True\n\n        self.logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n        self.checkpoint_path = os.path.join(\n            output_path,\n            'checkpoints',\n            f'{dataset_name}',\n            f'{self.seed}',\n        )\n\n        os.makedirs(self.checkpoint_path, exist_ok=True)\n\n        self.checkpoint_file = os.path.join(\n            self.checkpoint_path,\n            'checkpoint.pth'\n        )\n\n    def train_pipeline(self, data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor], load_checkpoint: bool = False):\n        self.iterations += 1\n        self.logger.debug(f'Starting iteration: {self.iterations}')\n        weights_changed = False\n\n        if load_checkpoint:\n            try:\n                self.load_checkpoint()\n            except FileNotFoundError:\n                self.logger.error(f'No checkpoint file found at: {self.checkpoint_file}'\n                                  f'Training the GP from the beginning')\n\n        self.model.train()\n        self.likelihood.train()\n        self.feature_extractor.train()\n\n        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([\n            {'params': self.model.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']},\n            {'params': self.feature_extractor.parameters(), 'lr': self.configuration['learning_rate']}],\n        )\n\n        X_train = data['X_train']\n        train_budgets = data['train_budgets']\n        train_curves = data['train_curves']\n        y_train = data['y_train']\n\n        initial_state = self.get_state()\n        training_errored = False\n\n        if self.restart:\n            self.restart_optimization()\n            nr_epochs = self.nr_epochs\n            if self.initial_nr_points <= self.iterations:\n                self.restart = False\n        else:\n            nr_epochs = self.refine_epochs\n\n        mse = 0.0\n\n        for epoch_nr in range(0, nr_epochs):\n\n            nr_examples_batch = X_train.size(dim=0)\n            if nr_examples_batch == 1:\n                continue\n\n            self.optimizer.zero_grad()\n\n            projected_x = self.feature_extractor(X_train, train_budgets, train_curves)\n            self.model.set_train_data(projected_x, y_train, strict=False)\n            output = self.model(projected_x)\n\n            try:\n                loss = -self.mll(output, self.model.train_targets)\n                loss_value = loss.detach().to('cpu').item()\n                mse = gpytorch.metrics.mean_squared_error(output, self.model.train_targets)\n                self.logger.debug(\n                    f'Epoch {epoch_nr} - MSE {mse:.5f}, '\n                    f'Loss: {loss_value:.3f}, '\n                    f'lengthscale: {self.model.covar_module.base_kernel.lengthscale.item():.3f}, '\n                    f'noise: {self.model.likelihood.noise.item():.3f}, '\n                )\n                loss.backward()\n                self.optimizer.step()\n            except Exception as training_error:\n                self.logger.error(f'The following error happened while training: {training_error}')\n                self.restart = True\n                training_errored = True\n                break\n\n        if training_errored:\n            self.save_checkpoint(initial_state)\n            self.load_checkpoint()\n\n    def predict_pipeline(\n        self,\n        train_data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor],\n        test_data: Dict[str, torch.Tensor],\n    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n        self.model.eval()\n        self.feature_extractor.eval()\n        self.likelihood.eval()\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            projected_train_x = self.feature_extractor(\n                train_data['X_train'],\n                train_data['train_budgets'],\n                train_data['train_curves'],\n            )\n            self.model.set_train_data(inputs=projected_train_x, targets=train_data['y_train'], strict=False)\n            projected_test_x = self.feature_extractor(\n                test_data['X_test'],\n                test_data['test_budgets'],\n                test_data['test_curves'],\n            )\n            preds = self.likelihood(self.model(projected_test_x))\n\n        means = preds.mean.detach().to('cpu').numpy().reshape(-1, )\n        stds = preds.stddev.detach().to('cpu').numpy().reshape(-1, )\n\n        return means, stds\n\n    def get_state(self) -> Dict[str, Dict]:\n        current_state = {\n            'gp_state_dict': deepcopy(self.model.state_dict()),\n            'feature_extractor_state_dict': deepcopy(self.feature_extractor.state_dict()),\n            'likelihood_state_dict': deepcopy(self.likelihood.state_dict()),\n        }\n\n        return current_state\n\nclass DyHPOAlgorithm:\n    def preprocess_hp_candidates(self) -> List:\n        log_hp_candidates = []\n\n        for hp_candidate in self.hp_candidates:\n            new_hp_candidate = []\n            for index, hp_value in enumerate(hp_candidate):\n                new_hp_candidate.append(math.log(hp_value) if self.log_indicator[index] else hp_value)\n\n            log_hp_candidates.append(new_hp_candidate)\n\n        log_hp_candidates = np.array(log_hp_candidates)\n\n        log_hp_candidates = self.scaler.fit_transform(log_hp_candidates)\n\n        return log_hp_candidates\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def patch_curves_to_same_length(curves):\n        max_curve_length = 0\n        for curve in curves:\n            if len(curve) > max_curve_length:\n                max_curve_length = len(curve)\n\n        for curve in curves:\n            difference = max_curve_length - len(curve)\n            if difference > 0:\n                curve.extend([0.0] * difference)\n\n        return curves\n\n    def acq(\n        self,\n        best_value: float,\n        mean: float,\n        std: float,\n        explore_factor: Optional[float] = 0.25,\n        acq_fc: str = 'ei',\n    ) -> float:\n        if acq_fc == 'ei':\n            if std == 0:\n                return 0\n            z = (mean - best_value) / std\n            acq_value = (mean - best_value) * norm.cdf(z) + std * norm.pdf(z)\n        elif acq_fc == 'ucb':\n            acq_value = mean + explore_factor * std\n        elif acq_fc == 'thompson':\n            acq_value = np.random.normal(mean, std)\n        elif acq_fc == 'exploit':\n            acq_value = mean\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError(\n                f'Acquisition function {acq_fc} has not been'\n                f'implemented',\n            )\n\n        return acq_value\n\n    def find_suggested_config(\n        self,\n        mean_predictions: np.ndarray,\n        mean_stds: np.ndarray,\n        budgets: List,\n    ) -> int:\n        highest_acq_value = np.NINF\n        best_index = -1\n\n        index = 0\n        for mean_value, std in zip(mean_predictions, mean_stds):\n            budget = int(budgets[index])\n            best_value = self.calculate_fidelity_ymax(budget)\n            acq_value = self.acq(best_value, mean_value, std, acq_fc='ei')\n            if acq_value > highest_acq_value:\n                highest_acq_value = acq_value\n                best_index = index\n\n            index += 1\n\n        return best_index\n\n    def calculate_fidelity_ymax(self, fidelity: int):\n        exact_fidelity_config_values = []\n        lower_fidelity_config_values = []\n\n        for example_index in self.examples.keys():\n            try:\n                performance = self.performances[example_index][fidelity - 1]\n                exact_fidelity_config_values.append(performance)\n            except IndexError:\n                learning_curve = self.performances[example_index]\n                lower_fidelity_config_values.append(max(learning_curve))\n\n        if len(exact_fidelity_config_values) > 0:\n            best_value = max(exact_fidelity_config_values)\n        else:\n            best_value = max(lower_fidelity_config_values)\n\n        return best_value",
    "Experiment Result": "The DyHPO method employs a Gaussian Process (GP) surrogate model with a deep kernel. The kernel's feature extractor (φ) is a neural network designed to process normalized budget and hyperparameter configurations through linear layers and learning curves through one-dimensional convolutions followed by global max pooling.\n\n**Feature Extractor (Neural Network φ) Architecture:**\n*   `nr_layers`: 2\n*   Input to first linear layer: Hyperparameters concatenated with budget (total `self.nr_features + 1` features).\n*   `fc1`: Linear layer with 64 units, followed by BatchNorm1d and LeakyReLU activation.\n*   Final linear layer (`fc2`): Takes input from the previous linear layer (64 units) concatenated with CNN features (4 channels), outputting 128 units, followed by LeakyReLU activation.\n*   CNN for learning curves: `Conv1d(in_channels=1, kernel_size=(3,), out_channels=4)`, followed by `AdaptiveMaxPool1d(1)`.\n\n**GP Model:**\n*   `GPRegressionModel` uses a `ConstantMean` function and a `ScaleKernel` with an `RBFKernel`.\n*   `GaussianLikelihood` is used for the GP.\n\n**Parameter Optimization:**\n*   Parameters are optimized via maximum likelihood (negative exact marginal log-likelihood) using the Adam optimizer.\n*   Learning rate for Adam: 0.001.\n*   Batch size for training the surrogate: 64.\n*   Number of epochs for full training: 1000, with an early stopping patience of 10 epochs.\n*   Number of epochs for refining (after initial training phase): 50.\n\n**Acquisition Function:**\n*   A Multi-Fidelity Expected Improvement (EIMF) acquisition function is used, specifically the Expected Improvement (`acq_fc='ei'`).\n*   The multi-fidelity aspect considers the best observed value (`best_value`) up to the current fidelity level (`budget`) for computing expected improvement.\n\n**Hyperparameter Optimization (HPO) Loop Settings:**\n*   Total HPO budget: 1000 evaluations (epochs/steps).\n*   Maximum budget for a single hyperparameter configuration in the benchmark (`max_benchmark_epochs`): 51.\n*   Fantasize step (budget increment per evaluation): 1.\n*   Initial configurations: 1 random configuration, evaluated at budget 1.\n*   Hyperparameter preprocessing: Log-transformation for specified log-sampled hyperparameters, followed by `MinMaxScaler` normalization across all hyperparameters.\n*   Learning curve preprocessing: Learning curves are patched (padded with zeros) to a uniform length determined by the CNN kernel size (3).\n*   Problem type: Maximization for LCBench (accuracy), Minimization for TaskSet (loss).\n*   Seed: Experiments are run with seeds from `np.arange(10)`, specifically `args.index - 1` is used.\n*   No Improvement Patience: If the best observed value (incumbent) does not improve for `max_benchmark_epochs + 0.2 * max_benchmark_epochs` iterations, the surrogate model is restarted from scratch."
}{
    "Title": "Provably Efficient Online Hyperparameter Optimization with Population-Based Bandits",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper introduces Population-Based Bandits (PB2), the first provably efficient PBT-style algorithm, addressing the limitations of Population Based Training (PBT) which lacks theoretical guarantees and requires vast computational resources. PB2 utilizes a probabilistic model, specifically time-varying Gaussian Process bandits, to guide the hyperparameter search efficiently, allowing discovery of high-performing configurations with significantly fewer agents than PBT. A key theoretical contribution is the derivation of the first sublinear regret bound for a PBT-style algorithm, demonstrating its efficiency and quantifying the gap between optimal and achieved rewards. PB2 is shown to be advantageous over existing batch Bayesian Optimization approaches by optimizing hyperparameters and learning their schedules in a single training run of parallel agents.",
    "Methodology": "PB2 formulates the online hyperparameter selection problem as batch Gaussian Process bandit optimization of a time-varying function. It models the reward function using a Gaussian Process with a time-varying kernel (specifically, a product form of squared exponential and a time kernel, `k_SE ◦ k_time`), which accounts for the non-stationary nature of neural network training. For parallel agents, hyperparameters are selected by sequentially maximizing an acquisition function (`µ_t,1(x) + sqrt(β_t)σ_t,b(x)`), which leverages knowledge of currently training agents to reduce redundancy and explore distinct regions. The algorithm periodically evaluates agents, copies weights from best performers to underperformers, and then uses the fitted GP model to select new hyperparameters for the underperforming agents by maximizing the acquisition function.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments primarily focused on Reinforcement Learning (RL) using OpenAI Gym environments (BipedalWalker, LunarLanderContinuous, Hopper, InvertedDoublePendulum) for Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) and Arcade Learning Environment (Breakout, SpaceInvaders) for IMPALA. A supervised learning task (CNN on CIFAR-10) was also used for generality. Population sizes ranged from B=4 to B=16 for PPO, B=4 for IMPALA, and B=4 for CIFAR-10. Tuned hyperparameters included batch size, learning rate, GAE parameter, PPO clip parameter for PPO; Epsilon, Learning Rate, Entropy Coeff for IMPALA; and Train Batch Size, Dropout values, Learning Rate, Weight Decay, Momentum for CIFAR-10. Baselines included Population Based Training (PBT), Random Search (RS), Bayesian Optimization (BO) with Expected Improvement, and ASHA. Experiments were validated by measuring the median best reward or accuracy across 10 (PPO), 7 (IMPALA), or 5 (CIFAR-10) random seeds, and interquartile ranges were plotted.",
    "Limitations": "The original Population Based Training (PBT) suffers from reliance on random heuristics for hyperparameter exploration, leading to the creation of meta-parameters that need tuning, and often underperforms random search without vast computational resources. PBT with small populations can collapse to a suboptimal mode due to its greedy nature and lacks theoretical guarantees. For PB2, while it addresses these PBT limitations, its theoretical regret bound holds under the condition that the batch size B is much smaller than the total time steps T (B << T). In the worst-case scenario where the time-varying function is not correlated (ω→1), PB2 achieves linear regret.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research directions include dynamically updating population sizes based on the value of the acquisition function. Another promising area is extending the search space to optimize broader aspects such as the selection of optimization algorithms or neural network architectures using Bayesian Optimization. Additionally, further gains in reinforcement learning experiments could be achieved by making more effective use of off-policy data.",
    "Experiment Code": "from GPy.kern import Kern\nfrom GPy.core import Param\nfrom sklearn.metrics import pairwise_distances\nfrom sklearn.metrics.pairwise import euclidean_distances\nimport numpy as np\nfrom copy import deepcopy\nimport itertools\nimport logging\nimport json\nimport math\nimport os\nimport random\nimport shutil\nimport GPy\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy.optimize import minimize\nfrom scipy.stats import norm\nfrom sklearn.metrics.pairwise import euclidean_distances, rbf_kernel\nfrom dppy.finite_dpps import FiniteDPP\nfrom ray.tune.error import TuneError\nfrom ray.tune.result import TRAINING_ITERATION\nfrom ray.tune.logger import _SafeFallbackEncoder\nfrom ray.tune.schedulers import FIFOScheduler, TrialScheduler\nfrom ray.tune.suggest.variant_generator import format_vars\nfrom ray.tune.trial import Trial, Checkpoint\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nclass TV_SquaredExp(Kern):\n    def __init__(self,input_dim, variance=1.,lengthscale=1.,epsilon=0.,active_dims=None):\n        super().__init__(input_dim, active_dims, 'time_se')\n        self.variance = Param('variance', variance)\n        self.lengthscale = Param('lengthscale', lengthscale)\n        self.epsilon = Param('epsilon', epsilon)\n        self.link_parameters(self.variance, self.lengthscale, self.epsilon)\n        \n    def K(self,X,X2):\n        if self.epsilon > 0.5: \n            self.epsilon = 0.5\n        if X2 is None: X2 = np.copy(X)\n        T1 = X[:, 0].reshape(-1, 1)\n        T2 = X2[:, 0].reshape(-1, 1)\n        dists = pairwise_distances(T1,T2, 'cityblock')\n        timekernel=(1-self.epsilon)**(0.5*dists)\n        \n        X = X[:, 1:]\n        X2 = X2[:, 1:]\n\n        RBF = self.variance*np.exp(-np.square(euclidean_distances(X,X2))/self.lengthscale)\n        \n        return RBF * timekernel\n    \n    def Kdiag(self,X):\n        return self.variance*np.ones(X.shape[0])\n    \n    def update_gradients_full(self, dL_dK, X, X2):\n        if X2 is None: X2 = np.copy(X)\n        T1 = X[:, 0].reshape(-1, 1)\n        T2 = X2[:, 0].reshape(-1, 1)\n        \n        X = X[:, 1:]\n        X2 = X2[:, 1:]\n        dist2 = np.square(euclidean_distances(X,X2))/self.lengthscale\n    \n        dvar = np.exp(-np.square((euclidean_distances(X,X2))/self.lengthscale))\n        dl =  - (2 * euclidean_distances(X,X2)**2 * self.variance * np.exp(-dist2)) * self.lengthscale**(-2)\n        n = pairwise_distances(T1,T2, 'cityblock')/2\n        deps = -n * (1-self.epsilon)**(n-1)\n    \n        self.variance.gradient = np.sum(dvar*dL_dK)\n        self.lengthscale.gradient = np.sum(dl*dL_dK)\n        self.epsilon.gradient = np.sum(deps*dL_dK)\n\nclass PBTTrialState(object):\n    def __init__(self, trial):\n        self.orig_tag = trial.experiment_tag\n        self.last_score = None\n        self.last_checkpoint = None\n        self.last_perturbation_time = 0\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return str((self.last_score, self.last_checkpoint,\n                    self.last_perturbation_time))\n\ndef normalize(data, wrt):\n    return (data - np.min(wrt, axis=0))/(np.max(wrt,axis=0) - np.min(wrt,axis=0))\n\ndef standardize(data):\n    data = (data - np.mean(data, axis=0))/(np.std(data, axis=0)+1e-8)\n    return np.clip(data, -2, 2)\n\ndef UCB(m, m1, x, fixed, kappa=0.5):\n    c1 = 0.2\n    c2 = 0.4\n    beta_t = c1 * np.log(c2 * m.X.shape[0])\n    kappa = np.sqrt(beta_t)\n    xtest = np.concatenate((fixed.reshape(-1, 1), np.array(x).reshape(-1,1))).T\n    preds = m.predict(xtest)\n    mean = preds[0][0][0]\n    preds = m1.predict(xtest)\n    var = preds[1][0][0]\n    return mean + kappa * var\n\ndef optimize_acq(func, m, m1, fixed, num_f):\n    opts = {'maxiter':200, 'maxfun':200, 'disp':False}\n    T=10\n    best_value=-999\n    best_theta = m1.X[0,:]\n    bounds = [(0,1) for _ in range(m.X.shape[1]-num_f)]\n    for ii in range(T):\n        x0 = np.random.uniform(0,1, m.X.shape[1]-num_f)\n        res = minimize(lambda x: -func(m, m1, x, fixed), x0, bounds=bounds, method=\"L-BFGS-B\", options=opts)\n        val = func(m, m1, res.x, fixed)\n        if val > best_value:\n            best_value=val\n            best_theta =res.x\n    return(np.clip(best_theta, 0, 1))\n\ndef get_diverse(X, y, size=10):\n    if X.shape[0] > 10:\n        size = int(X.shape[0]/2)\n    else:\n        return(X, y)\n    K = rbf_kernel(X, X)    \n    L = np.matmul(K, np.linalg.inv(np.eye(K.shape[0]) - K))\n    DPP = FiniteDPP('likelihood', **{'L': L})\n    DPP.flush_samples()\n    try:\n        DPP.sample_exact_k_dpp(size=size)\n    except ValueError:\n        return(X, y)\n    newX = X[DPP.list_of_samples[0], :]\n    newy = y[DPP.list_of_samples[0], :]\n    return(newX, newy)\n\ndef select_length(Xraw, yraw, current, newpoint, bounds, num_f, num):\n    min_len = 200\n    if Xraw.shape[0] < min_len:\n        return(Xraw.shape[0])\n    else:\n        length = min_len-10   \n        scores = []\n        while length+10 <= Xraw.shape[0]:\n            length += 10\n            base_vals = np.array(list(bounds.values())).T\n            X_len = Xraw[-length:, :]\n            y_len = yraw[-length:]\n            oldpoints = X_len[:, :num_f]\n            old_lims = np.concatenate((np.max(oldpoints, axis=0), np.min(oldpoints, axis=0))).reshape(2, oldpoints.shape[1])\n            limits = np.concatenate((old_lims, base_vals),axis=1)\n            X = normalize(X_len, limits)\n            y = standardize(y_len).reshape(y_len.size, 1)\n            kernel = TV_SquaredExp(input_dim=X.shape[1], variance=1., lengthscale=1., epsilon=0.1)\n            try:\n                m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n            except np.linalg.LinAlgError:\n                X, y = get_diverse(X, y)\n                m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n            try:\n                m.optimize(messages=True)\n            except np.linalg.LinAlgError:\n                X, y = get_diverse(X, y)\n                m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n                m.optimize(messages=True)\n            scores.append(m.log_likelihood())\n        idx = np.argmax(scores)\n        length = (idx+int((min_len/10))) * 10\n        return(length)\n    \ndef select_config(Xraw, yraw, current, newpoint, bounds, num_f, num):\n    length = select_length(Xraw, yraw, current, newpoint, bounds, num_f, num)\n    Xraw = Xraw[-length:, :]\n    yraw = yraw[-length: ]\n    base_vals = np.array(list(bounds.values())).T\n    oldpoints = Xraw[:, :num_f]\n    old_lims = np.concatenate((np.max(oldpoints, axis=0), np.min(oldpoints, axis=0))).reshape(2, oldpoints.shape[1])\n    limits = np.concatenate((old_lims, base_vals),axis=1)\n    X = normalize(Xraw, limits)\n    y = standardize(yraw).reshape(yraw.size, 1)\n    fixed = normalize(newpoint, oldpoints)\n    kernel = TV_SquaredExp(input_dim=X.shape[1], variance=1., lengthscale=1., epsilon=0.1)\n    try:\n        m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n    except np.linalg.LinAlgError:\n        X, y = get_diverse(X, y)\n        m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n    try:\n        m.optimize(messages=True)\n    except np.linalg.LinAlgError:\n        X, y = get_diverse(X, y)\n        m = GPy.models.GPRegression(X, y, kernel)\n        m.optimize(messages=True)\n    m.kern.lengthscale.fix(m.kern.lengthscale.clip(1e-5,1))\n    if current is None:\n        m1 = deepcopy(m)\n    else:\n        padding = np.array([fixed for _ in range(current.shape[0])])\n        current = normalize(current, base_vals)\n        current = np.hstack((padding, current))\n        Xnew = np.vstack((X, current))\n        ypad = np.zeros(current.shape[0])\n        ypad = ypad.reshape(-1,1)\n        ynew = np.vstack((y, ypad))\n        kernel = TV_SquaredExp(input_dim=X.shape[1], variance=1., lengthscale=1., epsilon=0.1)\n        m1 = GPy.models.GPRegression(Xnew, ynew, kernel)\n        m1.optimize()\n    time_param = m1.time_se.epsilon[0]\n    lengthscale = m1.time_se.lengthscale[0]\n    xt = optimize_acq(UCB, m, m1, fixed, num_f)\n    dists = euclidean_distances(X[:, num_f:], X[:, num_f:])\n    meandist = np.mean(dists)\n    min_new = np.min(euclidean_distances(X[:, num_f:], xt.reshape(-1,1).T))\n    xt = xt * (np.max(base_vals,axis=0) - np.min(base_vals,axis=0)) + np.min(base_vals, axis=0)\n    xt = xt.astype(np.float32)\n    return(xt, lengthscale, min_new, meandist)\n\ndef explore(data, bounds, current, base, old, config,           self._num_checkpoints += 1\n        else:\n            state.last_checkpoint = None\n\n        if trial in lower_quantile:\n            trial_to_clone = random.choice(upper_quantile)\n     "Experiment Result": "PB2 formulates online hyperparameter selection as batch Gaussian Process (GP) bandit optimization of a time-varying function. The method models the reward function (specifically, the reward improvement rate per timestep) using a Gaussian Process with a custom `TV_SquaredExp` kernel. This kernel is a product of a squared exponential kernel (for hyperparameters) and a time-decay kernel `(1-epsilon)^(0.5*dists)`, where `epsilon` controls the time-dependency.\n\nKey aspects of the PB2 implementation include:\n- **Data Preprocessing**: Input hyperparameters are normalized and rewards (improvement rates) are standardized (and clipped) before fitting the GP model.\n- **Dynamic Time Window**: The `select_length` function dynamically determines the length of the historical data window for GP fitting, selecting the length that maximizes the GP's log-likelihood to capture recent dynamics.\n- **Covariance Matrix Handling**: A `get_diverse` function uses a Determinantal Point Process (DPP) to sample a diverse subset of data, acting as a heuristic for ill-conditioned GP covariance matrices.\n- **Acquisition Function**: Hyperparameters for underperforming agents are selected by maximizing an Upper Confidence Bound (UCB) acquisition function, given by `mean + kappa * var`, where `kappa = sqrt(beta_t)` and `beta_t = c1 * log(c2 * N)` (with `c1=0.2`, `c2=0.4`, and `N` being the number of GP observations).\n- **Optimization**: The UCB acquisition function is optimized using `scipy.optimize.minimize` (L-BFGS-B method) with multiple random restarts.\n- **Parallel Execution**: PB2 integrates with Ray Tune's `FIFOScheduler`, operating on a population of parallel agents.\n- **Exploitation/Exploration Cycle**: Similar to PBT, PB2 periodically evaluates agents based on `time_attr` (e.g., `timesteps_total`) and `metric` (e.g., `episode_reward_mean`). Poorly performing agents (lower `quantile_fraction`) copy weights from top performers (upper `quantile_fraction`). Unlike standard PBT, hyperparameter mutation is replaced by the GP-guided selection (explicitly setting `resample_probability=0`).\n\nExperimental settings from `run_impala.py` and `run_ppo.py`:\n- **Framework**: Ray Tune.\n- **Algorithms**: IMPALA and PPO.\n- **Number of Samples (Trials)**: Typically 4 (`args.num_samples`).\n- **Evaluation Metric**: `episode_reward_mean` (maximized).\n- **Progress Metric (`time_attr`)**: `timesteps_total`.\n- **Perturbation Interval**: Varies (e.g., 500,000 timesteps for Impala, 50,000 for PPO).\n- **Quantile Fraction**: 0.25 (`args.perturb`).\n- **Resample Probability**: 0 (ensuring GP-guided selection).\n- **Hyperparameter Search Spaces (examples)**:\n    - **IMPALA**: `epsilon` (uniform 0.01-0.5), `entropy_coeff` (uniform 0.001-0.1), `lr` (uniform 1e-5-1e-2).\n    - **PPO**: `lambda` (uniform 0.9-1.0), `clip_param` (uniform 0.1-0.5), `lr` (uniform 1e-3-1e-5), `train_batch_size` (random integer within a specified range).\n- **Custom Exploration Function**: A post-processing `explore` function is applied to perturbed configurations to ensure parameter validity (e.g., `train_batch_size` constraints, integer casting)."

    "Title": "PriorBand: Practical Hyperparameter Optimization in the Age of Deep Learning",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the misalignment between existing Hyperparameter Optimization (HPO) algorithms and modern Deep Learning (DL) practice by proposing PriorBand. PriorBand is an HPO algorithm designed to leverage both expert beliefs and cheap proxy tasks, a feature missing in prior methods. Key contributions include the development of the first approach to combine cheap proxy tasks with expert prior input, a general HPO algorithm that satisfies all identified desiderata for DL, empirical demonstration of PriorBand's efficiency and robustness across a range of DL benchmarks, and highlighting the modularity of its Ensemble Sampling Policy (Eπ) for improving other multi-fidelity algorithms and model-based extensions.",
    "Methodology": "PriorBand extends HyperBand (HB) by replacing its uniform random sampling component with an Ensemble Sampling Policy (ESP) called Eπ. Eπ combines three sampling strategies: uniform random sampling (pU), prior-based sampling (pπ), and incumbent-based sampling (pˆλ). The algorithm dynamically adapts these proportions based on the optimization state. Specifically, the proportion of random sampling decays geometrically with increasing fidelity (rung), and the proportion of prior-based sampling increases. Incumbent-based sampling, which performs a local perturbation of the current best configuration (perturbing continuous hyperparameters with Gaussian noise N(0, σ^2) and discrete ones by weighted resampling), is activated after an initial budget and adjusts its weight against prior-based sampling by rating the likelihood of top-performing configurations under both the prior and incumbent distributions. The core problem is to minimize an expensive objective function f(λ) using a cheap proxy function ˆf(λ, z) at fidelity z, guided by a user-specified belief π(λ) about optimal configurations.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The research curated 12 diverse benchmarks for evaluation, including 4 PD1 benchmarks (e.g., lm1b_transformer, cifar100_wideresnet), 5 LCBench benchmarks from Yahpo-Gym, and 3 JAHSBench-201 benchmarks (e.g., CIFAR10, Fashion-MNIST). These benchmarks cover mixed-type search spaces, log-scaled hyperparameters, and various tasks such as language modeling, image classification, and tabular data. Baselines included representative HPO algorithms: HyperBand (HB), ASHA, asynchronous-HB (MF-RS), Bayesian Optimization (BO), πBO, BOHB, Mobster, and Random Search (RS), along with prior-augmented variants. Expert priors were generated in three qualities (near optimum, good, bad) using Gaussian distributions. Experiments were conducted with single-worker (50 seeds) and multi-worker (4 workers, 10 seeds) setups. Performance was evaluated based on average relative ranks and normalized regret of the anytime incumbent configuration at the maximum fidelity (zmax), typically for budgets of 12 full trainings (single-worker) or 5-20 function evaluations (multi-worker).",
    "Limitations": "PriorBand inherits known pathologies of HyperBand, such as susceptibility to poor fidelity correlations and sensitivity to the choice of fidelity bounds and early stopping rates. While the algorithm supports any prior distribution, experiments only used a Gaussian distribution with a fixed standard deviation (0.25). PriorBand is not entirely free of hyperparameters, such as the probability of perturbing hyperparameters (fixed at 0.5) and the standard deviation for continuous HP perturbations (fixed at 0.25). These were kept fixed in experiments to reduce confounding factors. Additionally, the experimental compute budgets (around 10-12 full trainings) may not always be feasible for all DL experts, although PriorBand aims for strong anytime performance even under shorter budgets.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work could explore dynamically adjusting PriorBand's hyperparameters, such as scheduling to reduce the standard deviation of Gaussian perturbation noise or dynamically adapting the probability 'p' for hyperparameter perturbation as optimization progresses to enhance exploitation. Leveraging expert insights into HPO landscapes could allow for more customized settings of the standard deviation for the Gaussian distribution or a tuned setting of 'p' based on knowledge of interaction effects or hyperparameter importance. The modular nature of PriorBand's Ensemble Sampling Policy (Eπ) also suggests its applicability and potential for further integration and improvement within other multi-fidelity algorithms.",
    "Experiment Code": "class RandomSearchWithPriors(RandomSearch):\n    use_priors = True\n\n    def __init__(self, prior_confidence: str = \"medium\", **optimizer_kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**optimizer_kwargs)\n        self.prior_confidence = prior_confidence\n        self._enhance_priors()\n\n    def _enhance_priors(self, confidence_score=None):\n        \"\"\"Only applicable when priors are given along with a confidence.\n\n        Args:\n            confidence_score: dict\n                The confidence scores for the types.\n                Example: {\"categorical\": 5.2, \"numeric\": 0.15}\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.use_priors and self.prior_confidence is None:\n            return\n        for k, v in self.pipeline_space.items():\n            if v.is_fidelity or isinstance(v, ConstantParameter):\n                continue\n            elif isinstance(v, (FloatParameter, IntegerParameter)):\n                if confidence_score is None:\n                    confidence = CUSTOM_FLOAT_CONFIDENCE_SCORES[self.prior_confidence]\n                else:\n                    confidence = confidence_score[\"numeric\"]\n                self.pipeline_space[k].default_confidence_score = confidence\n            elif isinstance(v, CategoricalParameter):\n                if confidence_score is None:\n                    confidence = CUSTOM_CATEGORICAL_CONFIDENCE_SCORES[\n                        self.prior_confidence\n                    ]\n                else:\n                    confidence = confidence_score[\"categorical\"]\n                self.pipeline_space[k].default_confidence_score = confidence",

    "Title": "Efficient Hyperparameter Optimization with Adaptive Fidelity Identification",
    "Main Contributions": "FastBO proposes a multi-fidelity Bayesian optimization (BO) method that adaptively determines the appropriate fidelity for each hyperparameter configuration to fit the surrogate model, addressing a key challenge in extending BO to multi-fidelity settings. This is achieved through the introduction of novel concepts: the efficient point, which signifies optimal resource-to-performance balance for surrogate model fitting, and the saturation point, which indicates when performance stabilizes at high fidelity. FastBO efficiently offers strong performance and can also extend any single-fidelity method to a multi-fidelity setting.",
    "Methodology": "FastBO's methodology is built upon two key concepts: the efficient point and the saturation point. The efficient point `ei` for a configuration `λi`'s learning curve `Ci(r)` is defined as the minimum resource level `r` where doubling resources `2r` yields performance improvement below a small threshold `δ1` (Ci(r) - Ci(2r) < δ1). This point serves as the appropriate fidelity for updating the surrogate model. The saturation point `si` is defined as the minimum resource level `r` beyond which observed performance variations are negligible (for all `r' > r`, `|Ci(r') - Ci(r)| < δ2`), serving as an approximate final fidelity. The FastBO process involves: 1) a warm-up stage to gather early observations and terminate poor configurations, 2) estimating the learning curve for each configuration, 3) adaptively extracting its efficient and saturation points, 4) evaluating the configuration up to its efficient point to update the surrogate model, and 5) a post-processing stage where a few promising configurations are further evaluated to their saturation points to identify the optimal one. This adaptive fidelity identification strategy is also generalized to extend single-fidelity methods to the multi-fidelity setting.",
    "Experimental Setup": "FastBO's performance was evaluated and compared against several baseline and state-of-the-art methods, including random search (RS), standard BO, ASHA, Hyperband, PASHA, A-BOHB, A-CQR, BOHB, DyHPO, and Hyper-Tune. The experiments were conducted using three established benchmarks: LCBench, NAS-Bench-201, and FCNet. The validation method involved assessing 'anytime performance' across these benchmarks, demonstrating FastBO's ability to handle various performance metrics and rapidly converge to the global optimum earlier than other methods.",
    "Limitations": "The paper implies limitations by stating challenges that demand future improvements. Specifically, FastBO's applicability and scalability need refinement and expansion to larger search spaces and distributed computing systems. The paper also mentions that it is an extended abstract, omitting finer details, which could be considered a limitation of the paper's comprehensiveness.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research could focus on refining and expanding FastBO to handle larger search spaces and integrate with distributed computing systems.     "Title": "Bayesian Optimization for Iterative Learning",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the problem of expensive hyperparameter tuning in deep (reinforcement) learning systems, which often involve iterative training and noisy evaluations, by proposing a Bayesian Optimization (BO) approach called BOIL. Key contributions include an algorithm that optimizes the learning curve of a machine learning algorithm by using training curve compression into a single numeric score based on training success and stability, rather than relying on averaged final performance. It introduces a method to learn the parameters of this compression curve (a Sigmoid function) directly from data by maximizing the Gaussian Process (GP) marginal likelihood. Furthermore, it presents a data augmentation technique that selectively includes informative intermediate training steps based on GP predictive uncertainty, avoiding the ill-conditioning issues caused by adding a full curve of observations. The algorithm demonstrates superior efficiency in identifying optimal hyperparameters for Deep Reinforcement Learning agents and Convolutional Neural Networks in minimal wall-clock time compared to existing baselines.",
    "Methodology": "The BOIL framework models the cost-sensitive black-box objective function, which depends on input hyperparameters and the number of training iterations, using a Gaussian Process (GP) with a product kernel combining similarities over both parameter and iteration spaces. The training time cost is approximated by a linear regressor. For selecting the next evaluation point, BOIL employs a cost-aware acquisition function that maximizes a modified Expected Improvement criterion divided by the predicted computational cost. A core component is the compression of the entire learning curve into a numeric score using a Sigmoid (Logistic) preference function, parameterized by growth and middle point values. These parameters are learned by maximizing the GP's log marginal likelihood via multi-start gradient descent. To augment training data efficiently without causing GP covariance matrix ill-conditioning, the method actively selects a subset of intermediate points from the observed learning curve that correspond to maximum GP predictive uncertainty, ensuring the natural log of the covariance matrix condition number stays below a predefined threshold.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The algorithm's efficiency was demonstrated by tuning hyperparameters for two Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) agents and one Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) across multiple environments and datasets. DRL tasks involved a Dueling DQN agent on the CartPole-v0 environment and Advantage Actor Critic (A2C) agents on the InvertedPendulum-v2 and Reacher-v2 environments. CNN tuning was performed on the SVHN and CIFAR10 datasets. All experimental results were averaged over 20 independent runs with different random seeds. The final performance of chosen hyperparameters was estimated over the maximum number of iterations. Experiments were conducted on an NVIDIA 1080 GTX GPU using the tensorflow-gpu Python package, with DRL implementations based on OpenAI Baselines. The GP used square-exponential kernels with parameters estimated by maximizing marginal likelihood. Key configuration parameters included a maximum of 15 augmented points and a threshold of 20 for the natural log of the GP condition number. Baselines for comparison included Hyperband and Continuous Multi-Task/Fidelity BO (CM-T/F-BO), along with ablation studies comparing to vanilla BO and BO-L (BO with compression).",
    "Limitations": "Existing iteration-efficient Bayesian optimization approaches often define objective functions as average performance over final training iterations, which can be misleading due to noise and fluctuations, especially in early training stages or with unpredictable DRL reward curves, rendering some early stopping criteria (like those in Freeze-thaw BO) inapplicable. Naïve data augmentation by adding full training curves can lead to redundancy and serious ill-conditioning issues in the Gaussian Process covariance matrix, particularly in noisy DRL settings. Hyperband, another baseline, is noted to be at a disadvantage in noisy DRL tasks because its early performance evaluations can be misled by fluctuations, and it lacks the flexibility to revise choices without resampling new hyperparameter pools. The cost function in BOIL is approximated by a linear regressor, which might not be appropriate if the cost has more complex dependencies. The paper also explicitly states that it omits comparisons with various acquisition functions, kernel choices, and Fabolas (designed for varying dataset sizes rather than iteration numbers), focusing instead on the effectiveness of its core compression and augmentation techniques.",
    "Future Research Directions": "The authors suggest that their framework is not limited to machine learning algorithms and can generally be applied to any process exhibiting an iterative structure that can be exploited, such as the optimization of manufacturing pipelines where factory settings are adjusted to increase productivity. The work is also seen as a step towards the construction of automated pipelines for the training and deployment of machine learning models. With the increasing deployment of such models, future work should focus on encouraging practitioners to implement necessary supervision and override mechanisms as precautions against potential failures, and to reflect on potential biases encoded in datasets and models. The paper also highlights the growing opacity of machine learning models as a concern that automated training procedures will contribute to, advocating for the continued development and rigorous use of work addressing the interpretability of trained machine learning models and their decision-making processes to ensure beneficial and transparent outcomes.",

1e-10 + 0 * var)\n        z = (mean - y_max)/np.sqrt(var2)        \n        out=(mean - y_max) * norm.cdf(z) + np.sqrt(var2) * norm.pdf(z)\n        \n        out[var2<1e-10]=0\n        return out\n ----------\n        xTest: the testing points \n        \n        Returns\n        -------\n        mean, var\n        \"\"\"    \n\n        if len(xTest.shape)==1: # 1d\n xTest=xTest[:,:-1]\n        \n        # prevent singular matrix\n        temp=np.hstack((self.X,self.T))\n        ur = unique_rows(temp)\n        \n        X=self.X[ur]\n        T=self.T[ur]\n
